===== .changeset/r22-trigger-framework.md =====
---
"@olympus-ai/core": minor
"@olympus-ai/triggers": minor
"@olympus-ai/vault": minor
"@olympus-ai/api": minor
"@olympus-ai/conformance": minor
---

The trigger framework (R22). Work enters the factory from a signed GitHub
webhook or a daily schedule as an intake candidate a person reads and may
admit. Nothing a trigger delivers starts a build or becomes an intent.

- `core`: `TriggerKind` gains `'issue'` (A-R22-01). `TriggerPolicy` gains
  `subscriptions`, `schedules`, `maxDeliveryAgeMs`, and `candidateExpiryMs`,
  and `taskTemplate` takes a `TemplateId` from the closed registry (A-R22-02).
  The validator refuses a non-human kind enabled without its whole route.
  Field schemas per kind, `checkFields`, and `admitTrigger`, the trigger check
  admission makes for every kind, `human` included.
- `triggers`: `TriggerEvent.extracted` is the kind's typed record (A-R22-03).
  The HMAC-SHA256 signature check, and the GitHub extractor, which is the only
  reader of `UntrustedPayload.raw`.
- `vault`: the intake store. A trigger decision and its candidate are one
  write under the body's digest. Refused-delivery counts, and the admitted and
  expired marks. `AdmissionRefusal` gains `trigger-refused` and
  `candidate-expired`, and `AdmissionRecord` names the candidate a run came
  from (A-R22-04, A-R22-05).
- `api`: admission reads the trigger policy, and `RunRequest.candidate`
  admits a run from a candidate, once. Routes: `POST /events/<subscription>`,
  the one route outside the local token, plus `GET /intake` and
  `GET /intake/<id>`. The daily schedule records missed firings. The host
  reads each subscription's secret from a `FACTORY_WEBHOOK_*` variable.
- `conformance`: the sink lint rule `i7/no-untrusted-sink`, and twelve trigger
  assertions. The three pending I7 entries are registered.

+/**
+ * Every credential the sandbox provider is given, by the name a relay spec
+ * names it with: the model key and the endpoint's, and nothing else. A
+ * subscription's secret is not among them, so no sandbox spec can name one
+ * and have it resolve (D-A-TF-05).
+ */
+export function providerCredentials(modelKey: string, endpoint: HostEndpoint | null): Record<string, string> {
+  return { [MODEL_CREDENTIAL]: modelKey, ...endpointGrants(endpoint).credentials };
+}
+
+/**
+ * Where the API server reads a subscription's secret: the host's own
+ * environment, and only a variable with the webhook prefix, so a policy
+ * cannot name the model key or any other host secret as one (D-A-TF-05).
+ */
+export function webhookSecrets(env: Readonly<Record<string, string | undefined>>): (name: string) => string | undefined {
+  return (name) => (name.startsWith(WEBHOOK_SECRET_PREFIX) && name.length > WEBHOOK_SECRET_PREFIX.length ? env[name] : undefined);
+}
+
 export async function composeHost(config: HostConfig): Promise<BuiltGraph> {
   const vault = new LocalVault({ store: config.store, artifacts: config.repository });
   const docker = config.docker === undefined ? {} : { executable: config.docker };
   const endpoint = config.endpoint ?? null;
   const grants = endpointGrants(endpoint);
   const sandbox = await LocalDockerProvider.create({
     ...docker,
     vaultPaths: [config.store, config.repository],
-    credentials: {
-      [MODEL_CREDENTIAL]: config.modelKey,
-      ...grants.credentials,
-    },
+    credentials: providerCredentials(config.modelKey, endpoint),
     dockerHostPorts: grants.dockerHostPorts,
   });
   const claude = new ClaudeCodeDriver({ provider: sandbox });
   // The endpoint's driver, in the seats the operator named and no others (D-R6-07). Its family is the operator's
   // declaration, so a reviewer or critic seated on it is graded against that declaration (I6).
   const other = endpoint === null ? null : new OpenAICompatibleDriver({ provider: sandbox, endpoint: endpoint.config });
   const seats = seatedDrivers<ClaudeCodeDriver | OpenAICompatibleDriver>(claude, other === null || endpoint === null ? null : { driver: other, seats: endpoint.seats });
   const seat = (name: EndpointSeat): ClaudeCodeDriver | OpenAICompatibleDriver => seats[name];
   const buildImage = await ensureImage(docker);
   return buildGraph({
     vault,
     sandbox,
     driver: seat('driver'),
     // With no endpoint, one driver in both seats: below L3 the seat is filled and run state records its independence as its grade (I6).
     reviewer: seat('reviewer'),
     // And in every drafting seat: a critic of its drafter's family is not seated, the condition is recorded as
     // failed, and every draft goes to a person (R19, D-A-DS-04). An endpoint of another family seats one.
     drafting: { specDrafter: seat('specDrafter'), specCritic: seat('specCritic'), testDrafter: seat('testDrafter'), testCritic: seat('testCritic') },
     workspaces: localWorkspaceStore(config.containerUser === undefined ? { root: config.trees } : { root: config.trees, user: config.containerUser }),
     integrator: config.git === null ? null : new GitHubIntegrator(config.git),
     profile: { buildImage, checkImage: config.checkImage ?? BASE_IMAGE, limits: config.limits ?? DEFAULT_LIMITS },
   });
 }

 /**

  * composition. Configured from its environment and nothing else, it touches

  *
  * - `FACTORY_API_TOKEN`: the local token every request must bear
  * - `FACTORY_POLICY_FILE`: the `policy.yaml` every run is admitted under
  * - `FACTORY_ROLES_DIR`: the roles directory every run's seats are compiled
  *   from, outside the repository and the trees (D-A-RC-01)
  * - `FACTORY_RUBRICS_DIR` (optional): the rubrics directory a drafting run's

 /**
  * The runtime as a service over HTTP (I9). It serves the run lifecycle —
  * create, status, approve, cancel, and an event stream — and nothing it does
  * reads a terminal, argv, or the process's streams: its host constructs it,
  * hands it the components and the local token, and tells it where to listen.
+ * Every route needs the local token except `POST /events/<subscription>`,
+ * which a delivery's signature authenticates instead (R22).

   // `spec` exit, naming the part (R17), and an acceptance manifest sits beside supplied tests or not at all (R19).
   const { specParts, acceptanceManifest, ...listed } = artifacts;

   const { spec, acceptanceTests, verificationManifest, taskGraph } = listed;

   if (!listOrDraft(spec) || !listOrDraft(acceptanceTests) || typeof verificationManifest !== 'string' || typeof taskGraph !== 'string') {

+
+/**
+ * Lets a refused delivery's bytes pass without buffering or parsing any of
+ * them, so the sender reads the answer rather than a reset; past `cap` more
+ * bytes the connection is cut, so a sender cannot hold it open.
+ */
+function discard(req: IncomingMessage, cap: number): void {
+  let seen = 0;
+  req.on('data', (chunk: Buffer) => {
+    seen += chunk.byteLength;
+    if (seen > cap) req.destroy();
+  });
+  req.resume();
+}
+
+/**
+ * Reads a delivery's body, held to `cap` while it is read: a body over it is
+ * never buffered past the cap, and is refused as oversize (D-A-TF-06).
+ */
+async function readCapped(req: IncomingMessage, cap: number): Promise<Buffer | 'oversize'> {
+  const declared = Number(req.headers['content-length'] ?? '0');
+  if (Number.isFinite(declared) && declared > cap) return 'oversize';
+  const chunks: Buffer[] = [];
+  let length = 0;
+  for await (const chunk of req) {
+    const buffer = chunk as Buffer;
+    length += buffer.byteLength;
+    if (length > cap) return 'oversize';
+    chunks.push(buffer);
+  }
+  return Buffer.concat(chunks);
+}
+
+/** A delivery's answer: 202 for what was recorded, and the cause's status for a refusal. */
+function deliveryStatus(outcome: DeliveryOutcome): number {
+  if (outcome.outcome !== 'refused') return 202;
+  switch (outcome.cause) {
+    case 'not-subscribed': return 404;
+    case 'unverified': return 401;
+    case 'oversize': return 413;
+    case 'replayed': return 409;
+    case 'budget': return 429;
+    case 'stale':
+    case 'schema':
+    case 'not-enabled':
+      return 422;
+  }
+}
+
+function summary(entry: IntakeEntry & { candidate: CandidateRecord }): CandidateSummary {
+  const c = entry.candidate;
+  return {
+    id: c.id, kind: c.kind, template: c.template, extracted: c.extracted, authorTrust: c.authorTrust,
+    lineage: c.lineage, recordedAt: c.recordedAt, admittedAs: entry.admittedAs, expiredAt: entry.expiredAt,
   };
 }

+
+/** 202 from `POST /events/:subscription`: what the delivery came to. A refusal is a 4xx with the cause, and no body text. */
+export type DeliveryAnswer = DeliveryOutcome;

===== packages/api/src/intake.ts =====
/**
 * How a trigger event becomes an intake candidate (R22). A delivery arrives
 * on `POST /events/<subscription>`, the one route outside the local token, and
 * is refused at the first check it fails:
 *
 * 1. the subscription is one the policy names, before the body is read (I4);
 * 2. the body is within its byte cap, held to it while it is read;
 * 3. its HMAC-SHA256 under the subscription's secret verifies, in constant
 *    time, before anything parses it;
 * 4. its digest is not one the Vault holds, so a body sent again is refused
 *    before it is parsed (D-R22-02);
 * 5. its own signed time is within the policy's age, and its fields are the
 *    kind's schema, checked whole (I7);
 * 6. the budget has room.
 *
 * The first three, and a replay, are counted per subscription and hour and
 * never written one by one: anyone can send them (D-R22-03). Every other
 * outcome is written once, under the body's digest, with its candidate in the
 * same write. A candidate starts nothing: a person reads it and may admit a
 * run from it through `POST /runs` (D-A-TF-01).
 *
 * The secret is resolved on the host, from a variable the subscription names
 * and only one with the webhook prefix (D-A-TF-05). No sandbox spec carries
 * it, and no driver request carries any byte of a body.
 */
import {
  WEBHOOK_SECRET_PREFIX, checkFields, type AuthorTrust, type CandidateId, type Policy, type RunId,
  type TemplateId, type TriggerLineage, type TriggerSchedule, type TriggerSubscription,
} from '@olympus-ai/core';
import { bodyDigest, extractGithub, signatureVerifies, untrustedBody } from '@olympus-ai/triggers';
import {
  intakeId, type AdmissionRecord, type CandidateRecord, type CountedRefusalCause,
  type TriggerDecision, type Vault,
} from '@olympus-ai/vault';
import { readIntegration } from './line.js';

/** Where a host's secrets come from. Only a name with the webhook prefix is ever asked for. */
export type SecretSource = (name: string) => string | undefined;

export interface IntakeOptions {
  readonly vault: Vault;
  readonly secrets: SecretSource;
  /** The runtime's clock, in ms. */
  readonly now?: () => number;
}

/** What a delivery came to, as the route answers it. Nothing here is the body's text. */
export type DeliveryOutcome =
  | { readonly outcome: 'candidate'; readonly candidate: CandidateId }
  | { readonly outcome: 'ignored' }
  | { readonly outcome: 'refused'; readonly cause: CountedRefusalCause | 'stale' | 'schema' | 'budget' | 'not-enabled' };

/** The UTC hour a count is kept under. */
export function utcHour(ms: number): string {
  return new Date(ms).toISOString().slice(0, 13);
}

/**
 * One delivery at a time per service: the budget is read and the candidate
 * written under one turn, so two deliveries cannot both take the last place.
 */
export class Intake {
  readonly #vault: Vault;
  readonly #secrets: SecretSource;
  readonly #now: () => number;
  #turn: Promise<unknown> = Promise.resolve();

  constructor(options: IntakeOptions) {
    this.#vault = options.vault;
    this.#secrets = options.secrets;
    this.#now = options.now ?? Date.now;
  }

  /** The subscription a delivery names, or null; null is counted as `not-subscribed` and the body is never read. */
  subscription(policy: Policy, id: string): TriggerSubscription | null {
    return (policy.triggers.subscriptions ?? []).find((s) => s.id === id) ?? null;
  }

  /** Counts a refusal that writes no record of its own (D-R22-03). */
  async count(bucket: string, cause: CountedRefusalCause): Promise<DeliveryOutcome> {
    await this.#vault.countRefusedDelivery(bucket, utcHour(this.#now()), cause);
    return { outcome: 'refused', cause };
  }

  /** Steps 3 to 6, for a body already read within its cap. */
  async deliver(policy: Policy, subscription: TriggerSubscription, signature: string | undefined, body: Uint8Array): Promise<DeliveryOutcome> {
    return this.#serial(async () => {
      const name = subscription.secret;
      const secret = name.startsWith(WEBHOOK_SECRET_PREFIX) ? (this.#secrets(name) ?? '') : '';
      if (!signatureVerifies(body, signature, secret)) return this.count(subscription.id, 'unverified');
      const digest = bodyDigest(body);
      if (await this.#vault.hasTriggerDecision(subscription.id, digest)) return this.count(subscription.id, 'replayed');
      const now = this.#now();
      const decide = (outcome: TriggerDecision['outcome']): TriggerDecision => ({
        source: subscription.id, key: digest, decidedAt: new Date(now).toISOString(), outcome, collectedBy: 'runtime',
      });
      const write = async (decision: TriggerDecision, candidate: CandidateRecord | null, result: DeliveryOutcome): Promise<DeliveryOutcome> => {
        // A key written between the check and here is the same body sent twice: the second is a replay.
        if (!(await this.#vault.recordTriggerDecision(decision, candidate))) return this.count(subscription.id, 'replayed');
        return result;
      };
      if (!policy.triggers.enabled.includes(subscription.kind)) {
        return write(decide({ kind: 'refused', cause: 'not-enabled' }), null, { outcome: 'refused', cause: 'not-enabled' });
      }
      // The validator requires the age while a non-human kind is enabled; absent, nothing is fresh (I5).
      const maxAge = policy.triggers.maxDeliveryAgeMs ?? 0;
      const extracted = extractGithub(subscription, untrustedBody(body, `github:${subscription.id}`), now, maxAge);
      if (!extracted.ok) {
        if (extracted.outcome === 'ignored') return write(decide({ kind: 'ignored' }), null, { outcome: 'ignored' });
        if (extracted.cause === 'stale') {
          return write(decide({ kind: 'refused', cause: 'stale', signedAt: new Date(extracted.signedAt).toISOString() }), null, { outcome: 'refused', cause: 'stale' });
        }
        return write(decide({ kind: 'refused', cause: 'schema', defects: extracted.defects }), null, { outcome: 'refused', cause: 'schema' });
      }
      if (await this.#overBudget(policy, now)) return write(decide({ kind: 'refused', cause: 'budget' }), null, { outcome: 'refused', cause: 'budget' });
      const template = policy.triggers.taskTemplate[extracted.kind];
      if (template === undefined) return write(decide({ kind: 'refused', cause: 'not-enabled' }), null, { outcome: 'refused', cause: 'not-enabled' });
      const id = intakeId(subscription.id, digest);
      const candidate: CandidateRecord = {
        id,
        kind: extracted.kind,
        template,
        extracted: extracted.extracted,
        authorTrust: extracted.authorTrust,
        lineage: await this.#lineage(extracted.commit, now),
        signedAt: new Date(extracted.signedAt).toISOString(),
        body: { sha256: digest, base64: Buffer.from(body).toString('base64') },
        recordedAt: new Date(now).toISOString(),
        collectedBy: 'runtime',
      };
      return write(decide({ kind: 'candidate', candidate: id }), candidate, { outcome: 'candidate', candidate: id });
    });
  }

  /**
   * A schedule's firing for a UTC date: a candidate whose one field is the
   * schedule's id, written once under `schedule:<id>` and the date. Its
   * author is the policy's, so its trust is `owner`.
   */
  async fire(policy: Policy, schedule: TriggerSchedule, date: string): Promise<DeliveryOutcome | null> {
    return this.#serial(async () => {
      const source = `schedule:${schedule.id}`;
      const now = this.#now();
      const decision = (outcome: TriggerDecision['outcome']): TriggerDecision => ({ source, key: date, decidedAt: new Date(now).toISOString(), outcome, collectedBy: 'runtime' });
      const write = async (d: TriggerDecision, c: CandidateRecord | null, result: DeliveryOutcome): Promise<DeliveryOutcome | null> => ((await this.#vault.recordTriggerDecision(d, c)) ? result : null);
      if (!policy.triggers.enabled.includes('scheduled')) return write(decision({ kind: 'refused', cause: 'not-enabled' }), null, { outcome: 'refused', cause: 'not-enabled' });
      if (await this.#overBudget(policy, now)) return write(decision({ kind: 'refused', cause: 'budget' }), null, { outcome: 'refused', cause: 'budget' });
      const fields = checkFields('scheduled', { schedule: schedule.id });
      if (!fields.ok) return write(decision({ kind: 'refused', cause: 'schema', defects: fields.defects }), null, { outcome: 'refused', cause: 'schema' });
      const id = intakeId(source, date);
      const candidate: CandidateRecord = {
        id, kind: 'scheduled', template: schedule.template satisfies TemplateId, extracted: fields.fields, authorTrust: 'owner' satisfies AuthorTrust,
        lineage: { depth: 0, chain: [], windowStart: new Date(now).toISOString() }, signedAt: null, body: null,
        recordedAt: new Date(now).toISOString(), collectedBy: 'runtime',
      };
      return write(decision({ kind: 'candidate', candidate: id }), candidate, { outcome: 'candidate', candidate: id });
    });
  }

  /** Records firings a stopped service missed, as one entry, and never runs them (D-A-TF-08). */
  async missed(schedule: TriggerSchedule, first: string, last: string): Promise<void> {
    await this.#serial(async () => {
      await this.#vault.recordTriggerDecision({
        source: `schedule:${schedule.id}`, key: `missed:${first}:${last}`, decidedAt: new Date(this.#now()).toISOString(),
        outcome: { kind: 'missed', first, last }, collectedBy: 'runtime',
      }, null);
    });
  }

  async #serial<T>(work: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
    const run = this.#turn.then(work, work);
    this.#turn = run.catch(() => undefined);
    return run;
  }

  /** Candidates recorded across every kind in the sliding window, against the policy's budget (D-R22-07). */
  async #overBudget(policy: Policy, now: number): Promise<boolean> {
    const { runs, windowMs } = policy.triggers.budgetPerWindow;
    const since = now - windowMs;
    let recorded = 0;
    for (const entry of await this.#vault.readTriggerDecisions()) {
      if (entry.candidate !== null && Date.parse(entry.candidate.recordedAt) > since) recorded += 1;
    }
    return recorded >= runs;
  }

  /**
   * An event naming a commit an Olympus run merged carries that run's chain
   * plus the run, one deeper. Read from the runtime's own integration and
   * admission records, never from the payload's say-so.
   */
  async #lineage(commit: string | null, now: number): Promise<TriggerLineage> {
    const windowStart = new Date(now).toISOString();
    if (commit === null) return { depth: 0, chain: [], windowStart };
    for (const runId of await this.#vault.listRuns()) {
      const merged = (await readIntegration(this.#vault, runId)).some((r) => r.kind === 'merged' && r.mergeCommit === commit);
      if (!merged) continue;
      const parent = await this.#parentLineage(runId);
      return { depth: parent.depth + 1, chain: [...parent.chain, runId], windowStart };
    }
    return { depth: 0, chain: [], windowStart };
  }

  async #parentLineage(runId: RunId): Promise<TriggerLineage> {
    const state = await this.#vault.readRunState(runId);
    const record = JSON.parse(new TextDecoder().decode(await this.#vault.read(state.admission))) as AdmissionRecord;
    return record.run.trigger.lineage;
  }
}

   switch (outcome.reason) {
     case 'invalid-request':
       return problemCodes(outcome.problems);
     case 'unsafe-above-l1':

+      return { reason: 'trigger-refused', cause: outcome.cause };
+    case 'candidate-expired':
+      return { reason: 'candidate-expired', candidate: outcome.candidate };

+/**
+ * Every role the run seats, with the scope policy grants it, as
+ * `policyRefusal` resolves them: a role the policy does not define is that
+ * check's refusal and is left out here.
+ */
+function seatedScopes(graph: TaskGraph, policy: Policy, drafted: readonly DraftStation[]): Array<readonly [RoleId, CapabilityScope]> {
+  const roles = new Set<RoleId>();
+  for (const station of M1_STATIONS) {
+    if (isDraftStation(station)) {
+      if (!drafted.includes(station)) continue;
+      roles.add(DRAFT_ROLES[station].drafter);
+      roles.add(DRAFT_ROLES[station].critic);
+      continue;
+    }
+    if (!isAgentStation(station)) continue;
+    for (const task of graph.tasks) if (task.station === station) roles.add(task.role);
+  }
+  return [...roles].flatMap((role) => {
+    const scope = Object.hasOwn(policy.roles, role) ? policy.roles[role] : undefined;
+    return scope === undefined ? [] : [[role, scope] as const];
+  });

+async function candidateFor(
+  vault: Vault, id: CandidateId, policy: Policy, now: number,
+): Promise<{ ok: true; entry: IntakeEntry & { candidate: NonNullable<IntakeEntry['candidate']> } } | Extract<AdmissionOutcome, { reason: 'invalid-request' | 'candidate-expired' }>> {

+    return { ok: false, reason: 'candidate-expired', candidate: id };

+  return { ok: true, entry: { ...entry, candidate: entry.candidate } };

 /** Every check admission makes, in order; `admitRun` records whichever refuses. */
 async function admission(req: RunRequest): Promise<AdmissionOutcome> {
   const problems = [...requestProblems(req), ...storeProblems(req)];
   if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems };
 
   const unsafe = unsafeComponents(req.components);
   if (unsafe.length > 0 && req.requestedLevel > 1) {
     return { ok: false, reason: 'unsafe-above-l1', requestedLevel: req.requestedLevel, unsafe };
   }
   const policy = checkedPolicy(req.policy);
   if (!policy.ok) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: [{ path: 'policy', code: 'invalid-policy', message: policy.message }] };
   const admitted = await admitArtifacts(req);
   if (!admitted.ok) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: admitted.problems };
   const drafted = draftedStations(admitted.admitted.drafted);
   const drafting = draftingProblems(req, admitted.admitted.drafted, policy.policy);
   if (drafting.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: drafting };
   // Where the run's drafts are written; a workspace that already holds it would have them mixed with what it holds (R19).
   if (drafted.length > 0 && (await existsIn(req.workspace, `${DRAFT_DIR}/${req.runId}`))) {
     return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: [{ path: 'workspace', code: 'exists', message: `the workspace already holds ${DRAFT_DIR}/${req.runId}, where this run's drafts are written` }] };
   }
 
   const capability = capabilityRefusalFor(req.components, drafted);
   if (capability !== undefined) return { ok: false, reason: 'refused', at: capability.at, transition: capability.refusal, state: null };
   const remote = remoteEgress(policy.policy, req.components.integrator ?? null);
   if (remote.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: remote };
   const refused = policyRefusal(req.requestedLevel, admitted.admitted.graph, policy.policy, drafted);
   if (refused !== undefined) return refused;
+  // The trigger policy binds every run, a person's own included (D-A-TF-02): a person's run is `human`, by
+  // the owner, at depth 0; a candidate's is its kind, its author's trust, and its lineage.
+  const admittedAt = Date.now();
+  let candidate: (IntakeEntry & { candidate: NonNullable<IntakeEntry['candidate']> }) | null = null;
+  if (req.candidate != null) {
+    const read = await candidateFor(req.components.vault, req.candidate, policy.policy, admittedAt);
+    if (!read.ok) return read;
+    candidate = read.entry;
+  }
+  const trigger: AdmittedTrigger = candidate === null
+    ? { kind: 'human', authorTrust: 'owner', depth: 0 }
+    : { kind: candidate.candidate.kind, authorTrust: candidate.candidate.authorTrust, depth: candidate.candidate.lineage.depth };
+  const triggerVerdict = admitTrigger(trigger, req.requestedLevel, seatedScopes(admitted.admitted.graph, policy.policy, drafted), policy.policy.triggers);
+  if (!triggerVerdict.ok) {
+    if (triggerVerdict.reason === 'trigger-refused') return { ok: false, reason: 'trigger-refused', cause: triggerVerdict.cause, detail: triggerVerdict.detail };
+    return { ok: false, reason: 'policy-refused', station: 'intake', role: triggerVerdict.role, refusal: triggerVerdict.refusal };
+  }
   const rolesAt = await rolesProblems(req);
   if (rolesAt.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: rolesAt };
   const roles = await admitRoles(req.roles, admitted.admitted.graph, drafted);
   if (!roles.ok) return roles;
   let rubrics: Partial<Record<DraftStation, ReadRubric>> = {};
   if (drafted.length > 0) {
     const directory = req.rubrics ?? null;
     const at = await rubricsProblems(directory, [['workspace', req.workspace], ['workspace store', req.components.workspaces.root]]);
     if (at.length > 0 || directory === null) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: at };
     const read = await readRubrics(directory, drafted);
     if (!read.ok) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: read.problems };
     rubrics = read.rubrics;
   }
   const worstCase = worstCaseCost(admitted.admitted.graph, policy.policy, drafted);
   if (req.requestedLevel > 0 && req.approvedCostUsd !== worstCase.usd) {
     const likely = await likelyCost(req.components, worstCase, policy.policy);
     return { ok: false, reason: 'cost-unapproved', requestedLevel: req.requestedLevel, worstCase, likely, approvedCostUsd: req.approvedCostUsd };
   }
 
   const { vault } = req.components;
   if (await hasState(vault, req.runId)) {
     return {
       ok: false,
       reason: 'invalid-request',
       problems: [{ path: 'runId', code: 'already-admitted', message: `run ${req.runId} was admitted before; resume it, do not start it again` }],
     };
   }
 
   // The base is what every workspace is built from, so it is taken before anything is recorded,
   // and discarded if admission refuses after it: a refusal leaves nothing behind.
   const { workspaces } = req.components;
   const baseTreeSha256 = await snapshotBase(workspaces, req.runId, req.workspace);
   // Drafted tests are bound at `test-design`, over the base with the draft in it (R19).
   const supplied = req.artifacts.acceptanceTests === 'draft' ? [] : req.artifacts.acceptanceTests;
   const unbound = await suiteBindingProblems(admitted.admitted.checks, basePath(workspaces, req.runId), supplied);
   if (unbound.length > 0) {
     await discardRun(workspaces, req.runId);
     return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: unbound };
   }
   // The security checks the base's stack must be scanned with, read from the base like the suite bindings (R17).
   const set = await adapterSetOf(basePath(workspaces, req.runId), req.components);
   const unscanned = securityProblems(admitted.admitted.checks, { secretScan: req.components.scanners.secretScan, staticAnalysis: set.staticAnalysis });
   if (unscanned.length > 0) {
     await discardRun(workspaces, req.runId);
     return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: unscanned };
   }
   const controls = unavailableControls(set, req.requestedLevel);
 
   const now = new Date().toISOString();
   const run: Run = {
     id: req.runId,
     repo: req.workspace,
     baseCommit: req.baseCommit,
-    trigger: { kind: 'human', eventId: req.runId, lineage: { depth: 0, chain: [], windowStart: now } },
+    trigger: candidate === null
+      ? { kind: 'human', eventId: req.runId, lineage: { depth: 0, chain: [], windowStart: now } }
+      : ({ kind: candidate.candidate.kind, eventId: candidate.candidate.id, lineage: candidate.candidate.lineage } satisfies TriggerRef),
     requestedLevel: req.requestedLevel,
     station: 'intake',
     graph: null,
     createdAt: now,
   };
   // The intent and each rubric are recorded before the admission record that names them (R19).
   // `draftingProblems` refused a drafted spec with no intent, so a drafted spec has one here.
   const intentText = admitted.admitted.drafted.spec ? (req.intent?.text ?? null) : null;
   const draftingRecords = async (): Promise<{ intent: AdmissionRecord['intent']; rubrics: RubricVersion[] }> => {
     const intent = intentText === null ? null : await vault.recordIntent({ runId: req.runId, text: intentText, from: 'principal', collectedBy: 'runtime' } satisfies IntentRecord);
     const versions: RubricVersion[] = [];
     for (const station of drafted) {
       const rubric = rubrics[station];
       if (rubric === undefined) continue;
       const ref = await vault.recordRubric({ runId: req.runId, station, text: rubric.text, sha256: rubric.sha256, collectedBy: 'runtime' } satisfies RubricRecord);
       versions.push({ station, sha256: rubric.sha256, ref });
     }
     return { intent, rubrics: versions };
   };
   const unrecorded: AdmissionRecord = {
     run,
     policy: policy.policy,
     roles: roles.roles,
     artifacts: admitted.admitted.artifacts,
     intent: null,
     rubrics: [],
     baseTreeSha256,
     unavailableControls: controls,
     // The same detection the controls and the security checks were read from (R18).
     stack: set.stack,
+    // The delivery the run came from, by the stored decision's hash (D-R22-03).
+    candidate: candidate === null ? null : { id: candidate.candidate.id, decisionSha256: candidate.sha256 },
   };
   const refusedControls = admissionRefusal(unrecorded);
   if (refusedControls !== undefined) {
     await discardRun(workspaces, req.runId);
     return refusedControls;
   }
+  // Taken last, before the record: a candidate admits one run, and the exclusive mark is what makes it once (A-R22-04).
+  // A record that then fails to write leaves the candidate spent, which refuses a second run rather than admitting one.
+  if (candidate !== null && !(await vault.markCandidateAdmitted(candidate.candidate.id, req.runId))) {
+    await discardRun(workspaces, req.runId);
+    return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: [{ path: 'candidate', code: 'already-admitted', message: `candidate ${candidate.candidate.id} admitted another run first` }] };
+  }
   const record: AdmissionRecord = { ...unrecorded, ...(await draftingRecords()) };
   const admission = await vault.recordAdmission(record);
   const state = await vault.commitRunState(
     {
       runId: req.runId,
       admission,
       station: 'intake',
       // The run enters its first station when it is admitted (A-R12-02).
       stations: [{ station: 'intake', enteredAt: now }],
       phase: 'working',
       tasks: {},
       attempts: {},
       results: {},
       evidenceRefs: [],
       violations: [],
       approvals: [],
       reviews: [],
       usage: [],
       cancelled: null,
       halted: null,
       drafts: {},
       version: '0',
     },
     '0',
   );
   return {
     ok: true,
     admitted: {
       state,
       worstCase,
       drive: (hooks: DriveHooks = {}) => runLine({
         run,
         policy: policy.policy,
         roles: record.roles,
         artifacts: lineArtifactsOf(record.artifacts),
         graph: admitted.admitted.graph,
         checks: admitted.admitted.checks,
         securityPart: admitted.admitted.securityPart,
         requirements: admitted.admitted.requirements,
         drafting: { drafted: admitted.admitted.drafted, intent: intentText, rubrics },
         components: req.components,
         baseTreeSha256,
         state,
         ...hooks,
       }),
     },
   };
 }

===== packages/api/src/schedule.ts =====
/**
 * The daily schedule (D-A-TF-08). Each schedule in policy fires once a day at
 * its UTC time as a `scheduled` event whose one field is its id. A firing the
 * service missed while it was stopped is recorded as missed, once, naming the
 * first and last dates, and never run late: catching up would burst the
 * budget on restart.
 *
 * The scheduler is the service's, not the CLI's (I9): it is started and
 * stopped by whoever constructs the server, and touches no terminal.
 */
import type { Policy, TriggerSchedule } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { Vault } from '@olympus-ai/vault';
import type { Intake } from './intake.js';

const DAY_MS = 24 * 3_600_000;

/** The UTC date of a time, `YYYY-MM-DD`. */
export function utcDate(ms: number): string {
  return new Date(ms).toISOString().slice(0, 10);
}

/** When `schedule` fires on the UTC date of `ms`. */
export function firingTime(schedule: TriggerSchedule, ms: number): number {
  const [hours = '0', minutes = '0'] = schedule.at.split(':');
  const day = Date.parse(`${utcDate(ms)}T00:00:00Z`);
  return day + Number(hours) * 3_600_000 + Number(minutes) * 60_000;
}

/** The latest date a schedule fired on or was recorded missed through, from its decisions; null if it never has. */
export async function lastCovered(vault: Vault, schedule: TriggerSchedule): Promise<string | null> {
  const source = `schedule:${schedule.id}`;
  let last: string | null = null;
  for (const entry of await vault.readTriggerDecisions()) {
    if (entry.decision.source !== source) continue;
    const date = entry.decision.outcome.kind === 'missed' ? entry.decision.outcome.last : entry.decision.key;
    if (last === null || date > last) last = date;
  }
  return last;
}

/**
 * The dates after `covered` whose firing time passed before `now`: what a
 * stopped service missed. A schedule that never fired misses nothing, since
 * there was no service to miss it.
 */
export function missedDates(schedule: TriggerSchedule, covered: string | null, now: number): { first: string; last: string } | null {
  if (covered === null) return null;
  const today = firingTime(schedule, now);
  const lastDue = today <= now ? utcDate(now) : utcDate(now - DAY_MS);
  const first = utcDate(Date.parse(`${covered}T00:00:00Z`) + DAY_MS);
  return first <= lastDue ? { first, last: lastDue } : null;
}

export interface Scheduler {
  stop(): void;
}

/**
 * Records what was missed, then sets a timer per schedule for its next
 * firing. The policy is read again at each firing, so a schedule removed from
 * it stops firing and one whose kind is disabled is refused and recorded.
 */
export async function startSchedules(
  policy: () => Promise<Policy | null>, vault: Vault, intake: Intake, now: () => number = Date.now,
): Promise<Scheduler> {
  const timers = new Set<ReturnType<typeof setTimeout>>();
  let stopped = false;
  const current = await policy();
  const schedules = current?.triggers.schedules ?? [];
  for (const schedule of schedules) {
    const missed = missedDates(schedule, await lastCovered(vault, schedule), now());
    if (missed !== null) await intake.missed(schedule, missed.first, missed.last);
  }
  const arm = (schedule: TriggerSchedule): void => {
    if (stopped) return;
    const at = now();
    const today = firingTime(schedule, at);
    const next = today > at ? today : firingTime(schedule, at + DAY_MS);
    const timer = setTimeout(() => {
      timers.delete(timer);
      void (async () => {
        const latest = await policy();
        const still = latest?.triggers.schedules?.find((s) => s.id === schedule.id);
        if (latest !== null && still !== undefined) await intake.fire(latest, still, utcDate(next));
      })().finally(() => { arm(schedule); });
    }, next - at);
    timer.unref();
    timers.add(timer);
  };
  for (const schedule of schedules) arm(schedule);
  return {
    stop: () => {
      stopped = true;
      for (const timer of timers) clearTimeout(timer);
      timers.clear();
    },
  };
}

===== packages/conformance/fixtures/lint/i7/untrusted-sink.ts =====
// I7 lint fixture for the sink rule. Each annotated line serialises untrusted
// text or widens it to a slot that keeps nothing of its type, both of which
// compile and pass the three coercion rules. The registry assertion lints this
// file with inline configuration ignored and requires each annotated line to
// report.
import type { UntrustedPayload, UntrustedText } from '@olympus-ai/triggers';

declare const payload: UntrustedPayload;
declare const raw: UntrustedText;
declare function sink(value: unknown): void;
declare function anySink(value: object): void;

// eslint-disable-next-line i7/no-untrusted-sink -- I7 fixture
export const serialised = JSON.stringify(payload.raw); // expect-lint i7/no-untrusted-sink
// eslint-disable-next-line i7/no-untrusted-sink -- I7 fixture
export const envelope = JSON.stringify(payload); // expect-lint i7/no-untrusted-sink
// eslint-disable-next-line i7/no-untrusted-sink -- I7 fixture
export const widened: unknown = raw; // expect-lint i7/no-untrusted-sink
// eslint-disable-next-line i7/no-untrusted-sink -- I7 fixture
sink(raw); // expect-lint i7/no-untrusted-sink
// eslint-disable-next-line i7/no-untrusted-sink -- I7 fixture
anySink(payload); // expect-lint i7/no-untrusted-sink
export function returned(): object {
  // eslint-disable-next-line i7/no-untrusted-sink -- I7 fixture
  return raw; // expect-lint i7/no-untrusted-sink
}
// eslint-disable-next-line i7/no-untrusted-sink -- I7 fixture
export const held: Record<string, unknown> = { raw }; // expect-lint i7/no-untrusted-sink
// eslint-disable-next-line i7/no-untrusted-sink -- I7 fixture

===== packages/conformance/lint/untrusted-sink.js =====
// I7: the sink rule (`I7.untrusted-sink-lint-rule`, D-F3-06, D-R22-09).
//
// The type system stops UntrustedText from reaching a string-typed slot, and
// three typed rules stop `+`, interpolation, and String(). Two paths remained:
// `JSON.stringify(raw)`, and widening `raw` to `unknown`, `any`, or `object`
// by annotation, argument, return, or property before casting, which hides it
// from the cast scan. This rule reports a value typed UntrustedText or
// UntrustedPayload in either place.
//
// It lives in the conformance package, a protected path, so weakening it needs
// the gate label as turning a rule off in eslint.config.js does.
import ts from 'typescript';

const UNTRUSTED = new Set(['UntrustedText', 'UntrustedPayload']);

/** Whether `type`, or any member of a union or intersection, is one of the two. */
function isUntrusted(type) {
  if (type.isUnionOrIntersection()) return type.types.some(isUntrusted);
  const name = type.aliasSymbol?.getName() ?? type.getSymbol()?.getName();
  return name !== undefined && UNTRUSTED.has(name);
}

/** A slot that keeps nothing of the value's type: `unknown`, `any`, `object`, or `{}`. */
function isOpaque(checker, type) {
  if ((type.flags & (ts.TypeFlags.Unknown | ts.TypeFlags.Any | ts.TypeFlags.NonPrimitive)) !== 0) return true;
  if (type.isUnion()) return type.types.some((t) => isOpaque(checker, t));
  if ((type.flags & ts.TypeFlags.Object) !== 0) {
    return checker.getPropertiesOfType(type).length === 0
      && checker.getSignaturesOfType(type, ts.SignatureKind.Call).length === 0
      && checker.getIndexInfosOfType(type).length === 0;
  }
  return false;
}

function isJsonStringify(callee) {
  return callee.type === 'MemberExpression'
    && !callee.computed
    && callee.object.type === 'Identifier' && callee.object.name === 'JSON'
    && callee.property.type === 'Identifier' && callee.property.name === 'stringify';
}

export const noUntrustedSink = {
  meta: {
    type: 'problem',
    docs: { description: 'Untrusted text is never serialised, nor widened to unknown, any, or object (I7)' },
    messages: {
      stringify: 'I7: {{type}} is passed to JSON.stringify, which serialises untrusted text into a string.',
      widened: 'I7: {{type}} is widened to {{target}}, which hides it from the cast scan.',
    },
    schema: [],
  },
  create(context) {
    const services = context.sourceCode.parserServices;
    if (services?.program == null) {
      throw new Error('i7/no-untrusted-sink needs type information; run it with a typed parser configuration');
    }
    const checker = services.program.getTypeChecker();
    const typeOf = (node) => checker.getTypeAtLocation(services.esTreeNodeToTSNodeMap.get(node));
    const nameOf = (type) => checker.typeToString(type);

    /** Reports `node` when it is untrusted and the slot it fills is opaque. */
    const checkSlot = (node) => {
      if (node == null || node.type === 'SpreadElement') return;
      const type = typeOf(node);
      if (!isUntrusted(type)) return;
      const contextual = checker.getContextualType(services.esTreeNodeToTSNodeMap.get(node));
      if (contextual !== undefined && isOpaque(checker, contextual)) {
        context.report({ node, messageId: 'widened', data: { type: nameOf(type), target: nameOf(contextual) } });
      }
    };

    return {
      CallExpression(node) {
        if (isJsonStringify(node.callee)) {
          for (const arg of node.arguments) {
            if (arg.type !== 'SpreadElement' && isUntrusted(typeOf(arg))) {
              context.report({ node: arg, messageId: 'stringify', data: { type: nameOf(typeOf(arg)) } });
            }
          }
          return;
        }
        node.arguments.forEach(checkSlot);
      },
      NewExpression(node) {
        node.arguments.forEach(checkSlot);
      },
      VariableDeclarator(node) {
        checkSlot(node.init);
      },
      AssignmentExpression(node) {
        checkSlot(node.right);
      },
      ReturnStatement(node) {
        checkSlot(node.argument);
      },
      ArrowFunctionExpression(node) {
        if (node.expression) checkSlot(node.body);
      },
      Property(node) {
        if (node.parent.type === 'ObjectExpression') checkSlot(node.value);
      },
      ArrayExpression(node) {
        node.elements.forEach(checkSlot);
      },
    };
  },
};

/** The plugin eslint.config.js loads: one rule, named for the invariant it serves. */
export default { rules: { 'no-untrusted-sink': noUntrustedSink } };

   'station:parked:iterations-exhausted': { kind: 'scenario', run: () => parkScenario('iterations-exhausted') },
   'station:parked:retries-exhausted': { kind: 'scenario', run: () => parkScenario('retries-exhausted') },
   'station:parked:starts-exhausted': { kind: 'scenario', run: () => parkScenario('starts-exhausted') },
   // R4: a conflict past maxConflictRetries 0 parks the task that met it, recorded by the line over that task at verify.
   'station:parked:conflicts-exhausted': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: () => conflictScenario('conflicts-exhausted', (decisions) => {
       const parks = decisions.filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'station-refused' && d.decision.refusal.reason === 'parked');
       const [park] = parks;
       if (parks.length !== 1 || park?.decision.decidedBy !== 'line' || park.taskId !== 'b' || park.station !== 'verify'
         || park.decision.cause !== 'station-refused' || park.decision.refusal.reason !== 'parked' || park.decision.refusal.cause !== 'conflicts-exhausted') {
         throw new Error(`P14: a conflict park was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(parks)}`);
       }
       // The conflict that parked it is recorded too, no later than the park. The Vault lists decisions by hash, so time orders them.
       const conflict = decisions.find((d) => d.decision.cause === 'task-conflict');
       if (conflict === undefined || conflict.decidedAt > park.decidedAt) throw new Error('P14: the park was recorded with no conflict before it');
     }),
   },
   'station:cancelled': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-cancel-', async (rig) => {
         const { cancelRun, resumeRun } = await api();
         const components = await rig.components();
         await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         const cancelled = await cancelRun({ runId: rig.runId, by: 'maintainer', vault: components.vault });
         if (!cancelled.ok) throw new Error(`P14: the cancel was refused: ${cancelled.message}`);
         refusalOf(await resumeRun({ runId: rig.runId, components }), 'P14 cancelled');
         await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'station:cancelled', { decidedBy: 'station-machine', taskId: null, station: 'integrate' });
       });
     },
   },
   'station:halted': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-halt-', async (rig) => {
         const { resumeRun } = await api();
         const components = await rig.components();
         await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         // What the service commits when a drive ends in an error (A-P9-02).
         const state = await rig.state();
         await components.vault.commitRunState({ ...state, halted: { at: new Date().toISOString(), message: 'the drive failed' } }, state.version);
         refusalOf(await resumeRun({ runId: rig.runId, components }), 'P14 halted');
         await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'station:halted', { decidedBy: 'station-machine', taskId: null, station: 'integrate' });
       });
     },
   },
   'approval-granted': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-granted-', async (rig) => {
         const { approveStation } = await api();
         const components = await rig.components();
         const key = await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         const approved = await approveStation({ runId: rig.runId, key: key as never, approvedBy: 'maintainer', vault: components.vault });
         if (!approved.ok) throw new Error(`P14: the approval was refused: ${approved.message}`);
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'approval-granted', { decidedBy: 'approval', taskId: null, station: 'integrate' });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'approval-granted' || d.decision.principal !== 'maintainer' || d.decision.key !== key) {
           throw new Error(`P14: the grant was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
         }
       });
     },
   },
   'approval-refused:invalid-request': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-approval-invalid-', async (rig) => {
         const { approveStation } = await api();
         const components = await rig.components();
         const key = await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         await approveStation({ runId: rig.runId, key: 'not a key' as never, approvedBy: 'maintainer', vault: components.vault });
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'approval-refused:invalid-request', { decidedBy: 'approval', taskId: null, station: null });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'approval-refused' || d.decision.key !== null) {
           throw new Error(`P14: a malformed key was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}; the caller's text is not stored`);
         }
         // A blank principal is refused too, and its key is kept because it is a key.
         await approveStation({ runId: rig.runId, key: key as never, approvedBy: ' ', vault: components.vault });
         const both = (await decisionsOf(rig, components.vault)).filter((x) => keyOf(x.decision) === 'approval-refused:invalid-request');
         if (!both.some((x) => x.decision.cause === 'approval-refused' && x.decision.key === key)) throw new Error('P14: a blank approver was not recorded with its key');
       });
     },
   },
   'approval-refused:not-awaiting': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-approval-early-', async (rig) => {
         const { approveStation } = await api();
         const components = await rig.components();
         await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         const refused = await approveStation({ runId: rig.runId, key: 'build:1', approvedBy: 'maintainer', vault: components.vault });
         if (refused.ok) throw new Error('P14: an approval of an exit the run is not at was granted');
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'approval-refused:not-awaiting', { decidedBy: 'approval', taskId: null, station: 'integrate' });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'approval-refused' || d.decision.key !== 'build:1') throw new Error(`P14: recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
       });
     },
   },
   'egress:opened': { kind: 'scenario', run: () => egressScenario('opened') },
   'egress:tunnelled': { kind: 'scenario', run: () => egressScenario('tunnelled') },
   'egress:refused': { kind: 'scenario', run: () => egressScenario('refused') },
   'violation-recorded': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: () => escapeScenario(async (rig, vault, state) => {
       const [violation] = state.violations;
       if (violation === undefined) throw new Error('P14: the escape recorded no violation');
       const before = await vault.read(violation);
       const d = await expectDecision(rig, vault, 'violation-recorded', { decidedBy: 'line', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'verify' });
       if (d.decision.cause !== 'violation-recorded' || JSON.stringify(d.decision.violation) !== JSON.stringify(violation)) {
         throw new Error(`P14: the violation decision points at ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}, not ${JSON.stringify(violation)}`);
       }
       if (Buffer.compare(Buffer.from(before), Buffer.from(await vault.read(violation))) !== 0) throw new Error('P14: the violation record changed');
     }).then(claimMismatchScenario),
   },
   'relay-refused': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-relay-', async (rig) => {
         const refusing: MeterReading = { kind: 'metered', calls: 3, inputTokens: 300, outputTokens: 30, cacheReadTokens: 0, cacheWriteTokens: 0, costUsd: 1, exhausted: 'cost', refused: 2 };
         const sandbox = await reportingProvider((n) => (n === DRIVER_CALL ? { meter: refusing } : {}));
         const components = await rig.components({ sandbox });
         await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         const [usage] = (await rig.state()).usage;
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'relay-refused', { decidedBy: 'model-relay', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'build' });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'relay-refused' || JSON.stringify(d.decision.usage) !== JSON.stringify(usage) || d.decision.refused !== 2 || d.decision.exhausted !== 'cost') {
           throw new Error(`P14: the relay's refusals were recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
         }
         // A call whose relay refused nothing records no relay decision.
         const relayed = (await decisionsOf(rig, components.vault)).filter((x) => x.decision.cause === 'relay-refused');
         if (relayed.length !== 1) throw new Error(`P14: ${String(relayed.length)} relay decisions for one refusing call`);
       });
     },
   },
   'task-conflict': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: () => conflictScenario('task-conflict', (decisions) => {
       const found = decisions.filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'task-conflict');
       const [d] = found;
       // Only the line may record one, which the Vault holds it to (A-R4-03).
       if (found.length !== 1 || d?.decision.cause !== 'task-conflict' || d.taskId !== 'b' || d.station !== 'verify'
         || JSON.stringify(d.decision.with) !== '["a"]' || JSON.stringify(d.decision.paths) !== '["parts/shared.txt"]') {
         throw new Error(`P14: a conflict was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(found)}`);
       }
     }),
   },
   'tier-escalated': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-escalate-', async (rig) => {
         const run = await failingRun(rig, { tier: 'fast', escalation: { afterFailedGates: 1, ceiling: 'standard' } });
         const vault = (await rig.components()).vault;
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, vault, 'tier-escalated', { decidedBy: 'line', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'build' });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'tier-escalated' || d.decision.from !== 'fast' || d.decision.to !== 'standard' || d.decision.failedGates !== 1 || run.decisions.length !== 1) {
           throw new Error(`P14: an escalation was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}, among ${String(run.decisions.length)}`);
         }
       });
     },
   },
 };
 
 /** The literals whose `reason` property is declared by `StationRefusal`, found in a package's own source. */
 /**
  * The string a literal's `reason` property is set to, however it is spelled:
  * a quoted key, and a literal inside parentheses, `as`, `satisfies`, or an
  * angle-bracket assertion, all count (external review of P14, codex-7).
  */

 /** I1: No agent writes to the Vault. */
 export const I1: InvariantEntry = {
   title: INVARIANTS.I1,
   assertions: [
     // R20: the role compiler.
     ...R20_I1,
     compileError({
       id: 'I1.write-boundary-vault-never',
       title: 'WriteBoundary.vault admits no value but "never"',
       fixture: 'i1/write-boundary-vault-never.ts',
     }),
     compileError({
       id: 'I1.mount-table-single-rw',
       title: 'MountTable admits at most one rw mount and only in the workspace slot: every other mount is ro by type, a second rw mount has no slot, and the workspace may itself be ro',
       fixture: 'i1/mount-table-single-rw.ts',
     }),
     compileError({
       id: 'I1.vault-has-no-generic-write',
       title: 'the Vault contract exposes named mutators only; write, put, and delete do not exist',
       fixture: 'i1/vault-has-no-generic-write.ts',
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I1.vault-implementation-exposes-only-named-operations',
       title:
-        'the filesystem Vault carries exactly the twenty-four named operations at run time: no generic write, no delete, and no helper left reachable on the prototype for a caller to mutate the store past them',
+        'the filesystem Vault carries exactly the thirty-two named operations at run time: no generic write, no delete, and no helper left reachable on the prototype for a caller to mutate the store past them',
       run: async () => {
         const { LocalVault } = await import('@olympus-ai/vault');
         const reachable = Object.getOwnPropertyNames(LocalVault.prototype).filter((name) => name !== 'constructor');
         const extra = reachable.filter((name) => !NAMED_OPERATIONS.includes(name));
         if (extra.length > 0) {
           throw new Error(
             `I1: the Vault exposes [${extra.join(', ')}] beyond its named operations. The type-level fixture cannot ` +
               'see these: a TypeScript `private` method is public at run time, so an internal helper is a write path ' +
               'the contract does not declare. Use a module-level function or a `#private` method.',
           );
         }
         const missing = NAMED_OPERATIONS.filter((name) => !reachable.includes(name));
         if (missing.length > 0) throw new Error(`I1: the Vault is missing the named operations [${missing.join(', ')}]`);
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I1.mount-layer-enforcement',
       title:
         'inside a provisioned container the mount table is what the kernel applies: the rw workspace is writable and its writes ' +
         'land on the host, a workspace the table marks ro is not writable, an `others` mount is not writable, and nothing ' +
         'written outside the workspace reaches any host path',
       run: async () => {
         await withProvider('i1-mount-layer-', async (provider, dirs, base) => {
           // The one rw mount is the workspace, and it really is the host's tree.
           await withSandbox(provider, specFor(dirs, 'rw'), async (handle) => {
             const wrote = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'echo landed > /workspace/made.txt']);
             if (wrote.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`I1: a write to the rw workspace failed: ${wrote.stderr}`);
             const onHost = (await readFile(join(dirs.workspace, 'made.txt'), 'utf8')).trim();
             if (onHost !== 'landed') throw new Error(`I1: the rw workspace write did not reach the host (read ${onHost})`);
 
             // Everything else the container can write is the container's own layer, and dies with it.
             const outside = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'echo escaped > /outside.txt']);
             if (outside.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error('I1: this assertion assumes the container root is writable; it was not');
             const leaked = (await readdir(base)).filter((name) => !DIR_NAMES.includes(name));
             if (leaked.length > 0) throw new Error(`I1: a write outside the workspace reached the host: ${leaked.join(', ')}`);
           });
 
           // A workspace the table marks ro is mounted ro, which is what lets verification run against a
           // tree it cannot modify (I3, and the TOCTOU limit P1 recorded against this unit's obligation).
           await withSandbox(provider, specFor(dirs, 'ro'), async (handle) => {
             const blocked = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'echo x > /workspace/nope.txt']);
             if (blocked.exitCode === 0) throw new Error('I1: a workspace the table marks ro accepted a write');
             if (!blocked.stderr.includes('Read-only file system')) {
               throw new Error(`I1: the ro workspace refused the write for the wrong reason: ${blocked.stderr}`);
             }
           });
 
           // And an `others` mount, which the type layer already holds to ro.
           const withOther = specFor(dirs, 'rw', {
             mounts: {
               workspace: { source: dirs.workspace, target: '/workspace', mode: 'rw' },
               others: [{ source: dirs.readable, target: '/readable', mode: 'ro' }],
             },
           });
           await withSandbox(provider, withOther, async (handle) => {
             const blocked = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'echo x > /readable/nope.txt']);
             if (blocked.exitCode === 0) throw new Error('I1: an `others` mount declared ro accepted a write');
           });
         });
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I1.mount-layer-refuses-a-vault-mount',
       title:
         'a mount that would put a Vault path in the container is refused before any container starts — declared directly, ' +
         `reached through a ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM}, or asked for as ro — and so is a second rw mount`,
       run: async () => {
         await withProvider('i1-mount-refusals-', async (provider, dirs) => {
           const direct = await refusalFrom(() =>
             provider.provision(
               specFor(dirs, 'rw', { mounts: { workspace: { source: dirs.vault, target: '/workspace', mode: 'rw' }, others: [] } }),
             ),
           );
           if (direct.layer !== 'mount') {
             throw new Error(`I1: a Vault path as the workspace was refused at the ${direct.layer} layer, not the mount layer`);
           }
 
           // The escape: the declared path is inside the workspace and only resolves to the Vault.
           // A containment check that ran before resolution would let this one through.
           const link = join(dirs.workspace, 'link');
           await linkTo(dirs.vault, link);
           const viaLink = await refusalFrom(() =>
             provider.provision(
               specFor(dirs, 'rw', { mounts: { workspace: { source: link, target: '/workspace', mode: 'rw' }, others: [] } }),
             ),
           );
           if (viaLink.layer !== 'mount' || !viaLink.message.includes(link)) {
             throw new Error(`I1: a ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM} into the Vault was not refused by name: ${viaLink.message}`);
           }
 
           // ro is refused too: a Vault inside the container is one flag away from a writable one.
           const readOnly = await refusalFrom(() =>
             provider.provision(
               specFor(dirs, 'rw', {
                 mounts: {
                   workspace: { source: dirs.workspace, target: '/workspace', mode: 'rw' },
                   others: [{ source: dirs.vault, target: '/audit', mode: 'ro' }],
                 },
               }),
             ),
           );
           if (readOnly.layer !== 'mount') {
             throw new Error(`I1: a Vault path mounted ro was accepted, or refused elsewhere: ${readOnly.message}`);
           }
 
           // The Vault reached by truncation rather than by naming it. Docker's --mount grammar
           // splits on commas, so a source of `<vault>,readonly` is mounted as `<vault>`: every
           // containment check passes on the longer name and the container gets the Vault. The
           // declared path carries no comma, so checking only what the caller wrote misses it.
           // Review finding 1; an I1 bypass demonstrated against a running container.
           const decoy = join(dirname(dirs.vault), `${basename(dirs.vault)},readonly`);
           await mkdir(decoy, { recursive: true });
           const plain = join(dirs.readable, 'plain-link');
           await linkTo(decoy, plain);
           if (plain.includes(',')) throw new Error('I1: this assertion needs a comma-free declared path');
           const truncated = await refusalFrom(() =>
             provider.provision(
               specFor(dirs, 'rw', { mounts: { workspace: { source: plain, target: '/workspace', mode: 'rw' }, others: [] } }),
             ),
           );
           if (truncated.layer !== 'mount') {
             throw new Error(`I1: a source resolving to a comma-bearing path was not refused by the mount layer: ${truncated.message}`);
           }
 
           // A second rw mount has no slot in the type, so a cast is how it arrives at run time.
           const twoWritable = {
             workspace: { source: dirs.workspace, target: '/workspace', mode: 'rw' },
             others: [{ source: dirs.readable, target: '/second', mode: 'rw' }],
           } as unknown as ReturnType<typeof specFor>['mounts'];
           const second = await refusalFrom(() => provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { mounts: twoWritable })));
           if (second.layer !== 'mount' || !second.message.includes('I1')) {
             throw new Error(`I1: a second rw mount was not refused by the mount layer: ${second.message}`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     external({
       id: 'I1.driver-executes-inside-the-sandbox',
       title: "a task's commands run inside the provisioned sandbox and nowhere else, and a driver with no provider cannot be constructed",
       level: 'runtime',
       package: '@olympus-ai/driver-claude-code',
       file: 'test/invariants.test.ts',
     }),
     ADAPTERS_EXECUTE_NOTHING_ON_THE_HOST,
     // I1a: the composed line, in real containers, mounts no Vault.
     COMPOSED_HOST_MOUNTS_NO_VAULT,
     // R6: the second driver's loop is on the host, and every tool and model call it makes is in the sandbox (D-R6-01).
     OPENAI_COMPATIBLE_EXECUTES_INSIDE_THE_SANDBOX,
   ],
   pending: [],
 };

         'the real engine refuses a role the policy does not define (capability-missing) and a station outside a defined role scope (station-forbidden); a granted station returns exactly the stated scope, so the refusals are rules rather than an empty policy',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         const undefinedRole = engine.resolveCapabilities(UNDEFINED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
         if (undefinedRole.ok) {
           throw new Error('I4: a role the policy does not define resolved to a capability scope');
         }
         if (undefinedRole.reason !== 'capability-missing') {
           throw new Error(`I4: an undefined role was refused as '${undefinedRole.reason}'; expected 'capability-missing'`);
         }
 
         const forbidden = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, FORBIDDEN_STATION, policy);
         if (forbidden.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: role '${GRANTED_ROLE}' resolved a scope at '${FORBIDDEN_STATION}', which its policy does not list`);
         }
         if (forbidden.reason !== 'station-forbidden') {
           throw new Error(`I4: a station outside the role scope was refused as '${forbidden.reason}'; expected 'station-forbidden'`);
         }
 
         // The control: an engine that refuses everything would satisfy both
         // checks above while granting nothing, so a granted station must
         // resolve, and to exactly what the document states.
         for (const station of GRANTED_STATIONS) {
           const granted = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, station, policy);
           if (!granted.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I4: role '${GRANTED_ROLE}' was refused at '${station}', which its policy grants (${granted.reason})`);
           }
           if (granted.scope.tools.join(',') !== GRANTED_TOOLS.join(',')) {
             throw new Error(`I4: the resolved scope at '${station}' carries tools [${granted.scope.tools.join(', ')}]; expected [${GRANTED_TOOLS.join(', ')}]`);
           }
           if (granted.scope.tools.includes(UNGRANTED_TOOL)) {
             throw new Error(`I4: the resolved scope carries '${UNGRANTED_TOOL}', which no role in the document grants`);
           }
           if (granted.scope.network.egress !== 'none') {
             throw new Error('I4: the resolved scope widened egress beyond what the document granted');
           }
         }
 
         // A grant is not a handle on the policy: widening what came back must
         // not widen what the next caller receives.
         const held = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
         if (!held.ok) throw new Error('I4: the control resolution was refused');
         try {
           held.scope.tools.push(UNGRANTED_TOOL);
         } catch {
           // Frozen, which is the intended arrangement.
         }
         const next = engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
         if (!next.ok) throw new Error('I4: the second resolution was refused');
         if (next.scope.tools.includes(UNGRANTED_TOOL)) {
           throw new Error(`I4: mutating a resolved scope added '${UNGRANTED_TOOL}' to what the policy grants`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.omitted-approval-is-human-required',
       title:
         'resolvePolicy fills every approval the document omits with human-required and never auto, while carrying a stated approval through unchanged, so the filling is a default rather than a rewrite',
       run: async () => {
         const { APPROVAL_KEYS, AUTONOMY_LEVELS, STATION_IDS, StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const stated = APPROVAL_KEYS.filter((key) => document.approvals[key] !== undefined);
         if (stated.length !== 1) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the fixture document must state exactly one approval for this assertion to mean anything; it states ${String(stated.length)}`);
         }
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(document);
 
         const expected = STATION_IDS.length * AUTONOMY_LEVELS.length;
         const keys = Object.keys(policy.approvals);
         if (keys.length !== expected) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the resolved policy carries ${String(keys.length)} approvals; the table must be total at ${String(expected)}`);
         }
         // Totality is proved by the count above; this loop is about what the
         // omitted keys were filled with.
         for (const key of APPROVAL_KEYS) {
           if (stated.includes(key)) continue;
           const outcome = policy.approvals[key];
           if (outcome !== 'human-required') {
             throw new Error(`I4: '${key}' was omitted by the document and resolved to '${outcome}'; an unconfigured approval must be human-required`);
           }
         }
         // The stated entry survives: a resolver that overwrote everything with
         // human-required would pass the loop above and be wrong.
         const [statedKey] = stated;
         if (statedKey === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the stated approval list has one entry and no entry in it');
         if (policy.approvals[statedKey] !== document.approvals[statedKey]) {
           throw new Error(`I4: the document stated '${statedKey}' and the resolved policy reports '${policy.approvals[statedKey]}'`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.empty-inventory-refuses-every-grant',
       title:
         'validateToolGrants against an empty inventory refuses every tool the policy grants, naming each with its role; an empty inventory is never read as allow-all',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine, validateToolGrants } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         const empty = validateToolGrants(policy, []);
         if (empty.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an empty inventory admitted ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length)} granted tool(s); an inventory offering nothing grants nothing`);
         }
         if (empty.ungranted.length !== GRANTED_TOOLS.length) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an empty inventory named ${String(empty.ungranted.length)} ungranted tool(s); expected all ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length)}`);
         }
         for (const tool of GRANTED_TOOLS) {
           if (!empty.ungranted.some((u) => u.tool === tool && u.role === GRANTED_ROLE)) {
             throw new Error(`I4: '${tool}' was granted to '${GRANTED_ROLE}' and the refusal does not name the pair`);
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a full inventory passes, so the refusal above is the
         // inventory doing work rather than the function refusing always.
         const full = validateToolGrants(policy, [...GRANTED_TOOLS]);
         if (!full.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: an inventory covering every grant was still refused (${full.detail})`);
         }
         // And one short of the grants refuses exactly the missing one.
         const [first] = GRANTED_TOOLS;
         if (first === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the granted tool list is empty');
         const partial = validateToolGrants(policy, [first]);
         if (partial.ok) throw new Error('I4: an inventory missing a granted tool was accepted');
         if (partial.ungranted.length !== GRANTED_TOOLS.length - 1) {
           throw new Error(`I4: a partial inventory named ${String(partial.ungranted.length)} ungranted tool(s); expected ${String(GRANTED_TOOLS.length - 1)}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.egress-entry-names-one-host',
       title:
         'an egress host list carrying a wildcard, a path, a prefix length, or whitespace is refused; a list of literal hosts is accepted, so the refusal is the entry being read and not the list being rejected',
       run: async () => {
         const { validatePolicyDocument } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const scope = document.roles[GRANTED_ROLE];
         if (scope === undefined) throw new Error('I4: the fixture document defines no granted role');
 
         // Refusing the bare string 'all' while accepting ['*'] would leave the
         // authored form reading as an explicit allowlist while denoting every
         // host. Each of these denotes a set, or is not a host at all.
         for (const entry of ['*', '*.example.com', '0.0.0.0/0', '::/0', 'https://example.com', 'exa mple.com']) {
           const widened = { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, network: { egress: [entry] } } } };
           const outcome = validatePolicyDocument(widened);
           if (outcome.ok) {

         const accepted = validatePolicyDocument(literal);
         if (!accepted.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I4: a list of literal hosts was refused: ${accepted.defects.map((d) => d.problem).join('; ')}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I4.role-lookup-ignores-the-prototype-chain',
       title:
         'a role the policy does not define is refused with capability-missing even when Object.prototype carries a scope-shaped value under that name, and a role named for a prototype member refuses rather than throwing; the resolved role map has no prototype',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
         const prototype = Object.prototype as Record<string, unknown>;
 
         if (Object.getPrototypeOf(policy.roles) !== null) {
           throw new Error('I4: the resolved role map has a prototype, so an undefined role can be answered by it');
         }
 
         // Pollution somewhere else in the process must not become a grant here.
         prototype[UNDEFINED_ROLE] = { stations: ['build'], tools: ['read'] };
         try {
           const outcome = engine.resolveCapabilities(UNDEFINED_ROLE, 'build', policy);
           if (outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(
               `I4: role '${UNDEFINED_ROLE}' is defined nowhere in the policy and resolved to a grant because ` +

                 'prototype chain is not.',
             );
           }
           if (outcome.reason !== 'capability-missing') {
             throw new Error(`I4: the polluted role was refused as '${outcome.reason}'; expected 'capability-missing'`);
           }
           if (engine.resolveAutonomy(1, 'build', UNDEFINED_ROLE, policy).ok) {
             throw new Error('I4: resolveAutonomy granted a level to a role that exists only on Object.prototype');
           }
         } finally {
           Reflect.deleteProperty(prototype, UNDEFINED_ROLE);
         }
 
         // A role named for a prototype member must refuse, not throw: an
         // exception is not the refusal the contract promises, and a caller that
         // catches it has no reason recorded.
         for (const name of ['__proto__', 'constructor', 'toString', 'valueOf']) {
           const role = name as typeof UNDEFINED_ROLE;
           let refused: string;
           try {
             const outcome = engine.resolveCapabilities(role, 'build', policy);
             refused = outcome.ok ? 'granted' : outcome.reason;
           } catch {
             throw new Error(`I4: an ungranted role named '${name}' threw instead of refusing with capability-missing`);
           }
           if (refused !== 'capability-missing') {
             throw new Error(`I4: an ungranted role named '${name}' resolved as '${refused}'`);
           }
         }

         if (!engine.resolveCapabilities(GRANTED_ROLE, 'build', policy).ok) {
           throw new Error('I4: the granted role was refused, so the guard is refusing everything');
         }
       },
     }),
     APPROVAL_OUTCOME_GATES_THE_STATION,
